\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}\left(\xi k+\frac{4\xi k|\lambda|}{|\tilde{\lambda}|}\right)B_{2}B_{1}\leq r^{-1}k\epsilon

(54)

due to our choice of
\xi
and Summary
1
parts (a) and (b).

In addition, due to Summary
1
parts (a), (b), (c), and our choice of
k
, for all
r\geq R
,
t\geq 0
, and
s\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle E_{y^{r}}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{B}^{r,k,t}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{y^{r}}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{B}^{r,k,t}}B_{2}e^{\frac{c}{2}|\hat{W}^{r}(s)|}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle P_{y^{r}}(\mathcal{B}^{r,k,t})^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{2}E_{y^{r}}\left[e^{c|\hat{W}^{r}(s)|}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{3}e^{-k\frac{B_{4}}{2}}B_{2}B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}\leq\epsilon.

So for
r\geq R
we have

\displaystyle E_{y^{r}}\left[\int_{t+\left(\xi k+\frac{4\xi k|\lambda|}{|\tilde{\lambda}|}\right)r^{-1}}^{t+kr^{-1}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq E_{y^{r}}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\left(\mathcal{B}^{r,k,t}\right)^{c}}\int_{t+\left(\xi k+\frac{4\xi k|\lambda|}{|\tilde{\lambda}|}\right)r^{-1}}^{t+kr^{-1}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad+E_{y^{r}}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{B}^{r,k,t}}\int_{t+\left(\xi k+\frac{4\xi k|\lambda|}{|\tilde{\lambda}|}\right)r^{-1}}^{t+kr^{-1}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}\left((1-\xi)k-\frac{4\xi k|\lambda|}{|\tilde{\lambda}|}\right)\epsilon r^{-\frac{1}{8}}+r^{-1}\left((1-\xi)k-\frac{4\xi k|\lambda|}{|\tilde{\lambda}|}\right)\epsilon\leq r^{-1}k2\epsilon.

(55)

Combining (
52
), (
54
), and (
55
), we have
that for
r\geq R
and any
t\geq 0

E_{y^{r}}\left[\int_{t}^{t+kr^{-1}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]\leq r^{-1}3k\epsilon.

We can now complete the proof. Consider first the discounted case.
For
r\geq R
we have

E_{y^{r}}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s\varsigma}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}e^{-l\varsigma\left(kr^{-1}\right)}E_{y^{r}}\left[\int_{lkr^{-1}}^{(l+1)kr^{-1}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]\\
\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}e^{-l\varsigma\left(kr^{-1}\right)}r^{-1}3k\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{1-e^{-\varsigma\left(kr^{-1}\right)}}r^{-1}3k\epsilon\\
\leq\frac{1}{e^{-\varsigma\left(kr^{-1}\right)}\varsigma\left(kr^{-1}\right)}3r^{-1}k\epsilon\leq\frac{3\epsilon e^{\varsigma\left(kr^{-1}\right)}}{\varsigma}\leq\frac{3\epsilon e^{\varsigma\epsilon r^{-\frac{7}{8}}}}{\varsigma}\leq\frac{3\epsilon e^{\varsigma\epsilon}}{\varsigma}

due to Summary
1
part (d). Taking
\epsilon=\frac{\varsigma e^{-\varsigma}}{3}\epsilon_{0}\wedge 1
proves the first statement in the proposition.

Consider now the ergodic cost. Let
T^{*}=kR^{-1}
. For any
t\geq 0
,
T\geq T^{*}
, and
r\geq R
we have

E_{y^{r}}\left[\frac{1}{T}\int_{t}^{t+T}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\frac{T}{kr^{-1}}\rceil}E_{y^{r}}\left[\int_{t+(l-1)kr^{-1}}^{t+lkr^{-1}}\tilde{d}\left(\hat{Q}^{r}(s)\right)ds\right]\\
\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\frac{T}{kr^{-1}}\rceil}r^{-1}3k\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{T}\left(\frac{T}{kr^{-1}}+1\right)r^{-1}3k\epsilon\leq 3\epsilon+\frac{3kR^{-1}\epsilon}{T^{*}}\leq 6\epsilon.

Taking
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}/6
completes the proof of the second statement in the proposition. ∎

8
Proofs of Some Stability Results

In this section we prove the key stability results, namely Proposition
7.1
, Lemma
7.2
, and Proposition
7.3
, that were used in the proof of Proposition
3.4
in Section
7.1
.

8.1
Proof of Proposition
7.1

We begin with the following auxiliary result which is proved in Section
9.3
.

Proposition 8.1
.

For any
c>0
and
\epsilon>0
there exist constants
B,R\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
T\geq 1
,
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
, and
r\geq R
we have

P\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil r^{2}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)\geq rc\}}v^{r}_{j}(l)\geq\epsilon T\right)\leq e^{-BT}

(56)

and

P\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil r^{2}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{u^{r}_{j}(l)\geq rc\}}u^{r}_{j}(l)\geq\epsilon T\right)\leq e^{-BT}.

(57)

We now complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Recall
\rho^{*}
introduced below Proposition
2.3
and
\theta
from Condition
2
.
Fix
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
and
y^{r}=(\hat{q}^{r},\hat{\Upsilon}^{r},\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r})\in\mathcal{Y}^{r}
. Let
\tilde{\upsilon}>0
,
\xi\geq 0
be given.
Let
\epsilon_{1}=\frac{\frac{|\theta_{i}|}{12}}{JC_{i}\left(1+\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}\right)}
,
\epsilon_{2}=\frac{|\theta_{i}|}{24JC_{i}}
, and
\epsilon_{3}=\frac{|\theta_{i}|\min_{j}\{\beta_{j}\}}{96J}
. Define the sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{r,T}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq r^{2}T}|A_{j}^{r}(t)-t\alpha^{r}_{j}|\leq\epsilon_{3}rT\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq r^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}T}|S_{j}^{r}(t)-t\beta^{r}_{j}|\leq\epsilon_{3}rT\right\}

\displaystyle\cap\left\{\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}_{j}^{r}(s)=1\}}ds\leq r\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}+\epsilon_{2}Tr\right\}

\displaystyle\cap\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil 2r^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta^{r}_{j}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)>r\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}v^{r}_{j}(l)\leq r\epsilon_{1}T\right\}

\displaystyle\cap\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil 2r^{2}\alpha^{r}_{j}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{u^{r}_{j}(l)>r\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}u^{r}_{j}(l)\leq r\epsilon_{1}T\right\}

and

\mathcal{A}^{r,T}=\cap_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{A}^{r,T}_{j}
.
Due to Propositions
6.2
(equations (
14
) and (
15
) with
c_{1}=1
and
c_{2}=0
),
8.1
, and
3.3
(and since
\kappa<1/4
) we know there exist constants
B_{1},B_{2},R\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
r\geq R
,
y^{r}\in\mathcal{Y}^{r}
, and
T\geq 1
we have

P_{y^{r}}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}^{r,T}\right)^{c}\right)\leq B_{1}e^{-B_{2}T},\;\;\epsilon_{3}T\leq r\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta^{r}_{j}T,\;\;\mbox{ and }\epsilon_{3}T\leq r\alpha^{r}_{j}T.

(58)

In addition, we assume
R
is sufficiently large that for all
r\geq R
we have
\frac{3\theta}{4}\geq r(K\rho^{r}-C)
,
\tilde{\upsilon}>Jr^{-1}
,
\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\geq Jr^{\kappa-1}c_{2}
,
2\min_{j}\{\beta^{r}_{j}\}\geq\min_{j}\{\beta_{j}\}
,and for all
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
and
t\geq 0
we have
x^{r}_{j}(t)\geq\frac{\rho^{*}}{4}
(see Proposition
2.6
part (c)). For the rest of the proof we will restrict ourselves to values of
r
satisfying
r\geq R
. Note that if
\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(s)\geq\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\geq Jc_{2}r^{\kappa-1}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}=0
then due to Proposition
2.6
part (d) we have

(K\mathfrak{b}^{r})_{i}(r^{2}s)=C_{i}
.
This implies that if

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}=0
,

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}=0,\mbox{ and }\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(s)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)\geq\tilde{\upsilon}

then

(K\mathfrak{b}^{r})_{i}(r^{2}s)=C_{i}
.
Define

\hat{t}\doteq\sup\{t\in[0,\xi]:\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(t)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(t)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(t)<\tilde{\upsilon}\}

with the convention that if

\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(t)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(t)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)\geq\tilde{\upsilon}\mbox{ for all }t\in[0,\xi]\mbox{ then }\hat{t}\doteq 0.

Define

T^{*}\doteq 1+2\xi+\frac{4}{|\theta_{i}|}\left(\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}+\left(C_{i}+1\right)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}\right)

and let
T>T^{*}
. Then on the set
\left\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right\}\cap\mathcal{A}^{r,T}
, for all
s\in[\hat{t},T]
, we have

\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(s)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)\geq\tilde{\upsilon}

so for any
s\in[\hat{t},T]
satisfying

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}=0,

and

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}=0,

we have

(K\mathfrak{b}^{r})_{i}(s)=C_{i}
.
Consequently, on the set
\left\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right\}\cap\mathcal{A}^{r,T}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\left(\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(T)-\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(\hat{t})\right)=\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\mathfrak{b}^{r}_{j}(r^{2}s)ds

\displaystyle\geq C_{i}(T-\hat{t})-C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}ds

\displaystyle\quad-C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}ds-C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{\hat{t}}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}ds

\displaystyle\geq C_{i}(T-\hat{t})-C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}ds

\displaystyle\quad-C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}ds-C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}\}}ds.

(59)

For all
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\right\}}ds

\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(T)}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}v^{r}_{j}(k)\leq B^{r}_{j}(s)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}v^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds.

Recall that due to Proposition
2.6
part (c) and our assumption on the size of
r
we have
\mathfrak{b}^{r}_{j}(s)\geq\frac{\rho^{*}}{4}
unless
\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=1
so for all
l\in\{1,...,\tau^{r,S}_{j}(T)\}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}v^{r}_{j}(k)\leq B^{r}_{j}(s)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}v^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=0\}}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}v^{r}_{j}(k)\leq B^{r}_{j}(s)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}v^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}v^{r}_{j}(k)\leq B^{r}_{j}(s)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}v^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}v^{r}_{j}(l)+\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}v^{r}_{j}(k)\leq B^{r}_{j}(s)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}v^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds.

Combining these last two inequalities gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\right\}}ds\leq r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(T)}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}v^{r}_{j}(l)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(T)}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}v^{r}_{j}(k)\leq B^{r}_{j}(s)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}v^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds

\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(T)}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}v^{r}_{j}(l)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}ds.

Note that on the set
\mathcal{A}^{r,T}
we have
\tau^{r,S}_{j}(T)\leq\lceil 2r^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta^{r}_{j}T\rceil
and

\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil 2r^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta^{r}_{j}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}v^{r}_{j}(l)\leq r^{-1}\epsilon_{1}T

which gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}(s)\geq\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\right\}}ds=r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+r^{-1}\frac{4\epsilon_{1}}{\rho^{*}}T+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{r}_{j}(s)=1\}}ds.

Also, on the set
\mathcal{A}^{r,T}
, we have
\tau^{r,A}_{j}(T)\leq\lceil 2r^{2}\alpha^{r}_{j}T\rceil
and

\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil 2r^{2}\alpha^{r}_{j}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{u^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}u^{r}_{j}(l)\leq r^{-1}\epsilon_{1}T

so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}(s)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\right\}}ds

\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}+\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,A}_{j}(T)}\mathcal{I}_{\{u^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{0}^{r^{2}T}\mathcal{I}_{\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}u^{r}_{j}(k)\leq s\leq\sum_{k=1}^{l}u^{r}_{j}(k)\right\}}ds

\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,A}_{j}(T)}\mathcal{I}_{\{u^{r}_{j}(l)>\frac{\tilde{\upsilon}}{2J+1}r\}}u^{r}_{j}(l)\leq r^{-1}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}+r^{-1}\epsilon_{1}T.

Thus from (
59
), on
\left\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right\}\cap\mathcal{A}^{r,T}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\left(\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(T)-\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(\hat{t})\right)

\displaystyle\geq C_{i}(T-\hat{t})-2C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{\{\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(r^{2}s)=1\}}ds-r^{-1}JC_{i}\epsilon_{1}T\left(1+\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-r^{-1}C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}-r^{-1}C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}

\displaystyle\geq C_{i}(T-\hat{t})-r^{-1}C_{i}J2\epsilon_{2}T-r^{-1}JC_{i}\epsilon_{1}T\left(1+\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-r^{-1}C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}-3r^{-1}C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}.

(60)

Consequently on
\left\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right\}\cap\mathcal{A}^{r,T}
we have

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(T)

\displaystyle\leq\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(\hat{t})+Jr^{-1}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{\beta^{r}_{j}}\left(\hat{A}^{r}_{j}\left(\left(T-\bar{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}\right)^{+}\right)-\hat{A}^{r}_{j}\left(\left(\hat{t}-\bar{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}\right)^{+}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{\beta^{r}_{j}}\left(\hat{S}^{r}_{j}\left(\left(\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(T)-\bar{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}\right)^{+}\right)-\hat{S}^{r}_{j}\left(\left(\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(\hat{t})-\bar{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}\right)^{+}\right)\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}

\displaystyle\quad+(T-\hat{t})r((K\rho^{r})_{i}-C_{i})+r\left(C_{i}(T-\hat{t})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}(\bar{B}^{r}(T)-\bar{B}^{r}(\hat{t}))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(\hat{t})+\tilde{\upsilon}+\frac{4J}{\min_{j}\{\beta^{r}_{j}\}}\epsilon_{3}T+\frac{3\theta_{i}}{4}(T-\hat{t})+C_{i}J2\epsilon_{2}T

\displaystyle\quad+JC_{i}\epsilon_{1}T\left(1+\frac{4}{\rho^{*}}\right)+(C_{i}+1)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}.

Note that
\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(\hat{t})\leq\max\{\hat{w}^{r}_{i},\tilde{\upsilon}+r^{-1}J\}\leq\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+2\tilde{\upsilon}
which, combined with our choices of
\epsilon_{i}
, gives

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(T)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}-\frac{\theta_{i}}{12}T+\frac{3\theta_{i}}{4}(T-\hat{t})-\frac{\theta_{i}}{12}T-\frac{\theta_{i}}{12}T+(C_{i}+1)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}+\frac{3\theta_{i}}{4}(T-\xi)-\frac{\theta_{i}}{4}T+(C_{i}+1)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}+(C_{i}+1)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S,r}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A,r}_{j}+\frac{\theta_{i}}{4}(T-\xi),

where in the last inequality we have used
T<2(T-\xi)
which follows from our choice of
T
.
Since
T>\xi+\frac{4}{|\theta_{i}|}\left(\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}+\left(C_{i}+1\right)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\right)

and
\theta_{i}<0
this implies

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(T)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\hat{w}^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}+\left(C_{i}+1\right)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\theta_{i}\left(-\frac{4}{\theta_{i}}\left(w^{r}_{i}+3\tilde{\upsilon}+\left(C_{i}+1\right)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{S}_{j}+3C_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\right)\right)=0

which contradicts the fact that
\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(t)\geq 0
for all
t\geq 0
. Therefore for all
T>T^{*}
we have
\left\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right\}\cap\mathcal{A}^{r,T}=\emptyset
which says that
\left\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right\}\subset\left(\mathcal{A}^{r,T}\right)^{c}
and consequently from (
58
)

P_{y^{r}}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>T\right)\leq P_{y^{r}}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}^{r,T}\right)^{c}\right)\leq B_{1}e^{-B_{2}T}
.
Finally, for any
c\in(0,\frac{1}{2}B_{2}]
and
r\geq R
we have

\displaystyle E_{y^{r}}[e^{c\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{cT^{*}}+\int_{\left(e^{cT^{*}},\infty\right)}P_{y^{r}}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,\xi}>\frac{\ln(x)}{c}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{cT^{*}}+\int_{\left(e^{cT^{*}},\infty\right)}B_{1}e^{-\frac{B_{2}\ln(x)}{c}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{\frac{1}{2}B_{2}T^{*}}+B_{1}.

The result follows on recalling the definition of
T^{*}
.
∎

8.2
Proof of Lemma

7.2

Since
\hat{Y}^{r}(\cdot)
is a
\mathcal{G}^{r}(t)
Markov process, we have, for
0\leq s<t
,

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(t)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,t}-t)}\right]=E_{y}\left[E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,t}-t)}|\mathcal{G}^{r}(s)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[E_{\hat{Y}^{r}(s)}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,t-s}-(t-s))}\right]\right]=E_{y}\left[E_{\hat{Y}^{r}(s)}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(t-s)\right)\right]\right].

Proposition 8.2
.

For
\tilde{\upsilon}>0
let
\tilde{\delta}
be as in Proposition
7.1
. There exist constants
\tilde{R},\tilde{B}\in(0,\infty)
such that for any
y=(\hat{q},\hat{\Upsilon},\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{Y}^{r}
,
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
,
s\in[0,1]
, and
r\geq\tilde{R}
we have

E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(s)\right)\right]\leq e^{-\tilde{\delta}s}V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(y\right)+\tilde{B}.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\upsilon}>0
be fixed, let
B_{1},B_{2},B_{3},R
be as in Theorem
7.1
, and let
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{r}
,
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
, and
s\in[0,1]
be arbitrary. Due to the fact that
\hat{Y}^{r}(t)
is a
\mathcal{G}^{r}(t)
strong Markov process and
\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}\wedge s
is a bounded
\mathcal{G}^{r}(t)
stopping time, for all
r\geq R
we have,

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(s)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,s}-s)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}\geq s\}}e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,s}-s)}\right]+E_{y}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}<s\}}e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,s}-s)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}-s)}\right]+E_{y}\left[E_{y}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}<s\}}e^{\tilde{\delta}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,s}-s)}|\mathcal{G}^{r}(\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}\wedge s)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\tilde{\delta}s}E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}}\right]+\sup_{(y,z):w_{i}+|\hat{\Upsilon}|\leq\tilde{\upsilon},z\in[0,s]}\left\{E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,z}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\tilde{\delta}s}V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}(y)+B_{1}e^{B_{2}\left(1+2\tilde{\upsilon}\right)}+B_{3}

where the last line uses Proposition
7.1
. This completes the proof.
∎

We now complete the proof of Lemma
7.2
.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.
Let
\tilde{\upsilon}>0
be fixed and note that, with
\tilde{\delta}
as in Proposition
7.1
, due to Proposition
8.2
, there exist constants
B_{1},R\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{r}
,
r\geq R
,
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
, and
t\in[0,1]
we have

E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(t)\right)\right]\leq e^{-\tilde{\delta}t}V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(y\right)+B_{1}.

Consequently for
T\in[0,1]
we already have the result (with
B=B_{1}
). Let
T>1
be arbitrary and note that there exists
t\in\left[\frac{1}{2},1\right]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
T=nt
. Using the Markov property we have

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(T)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(nt)\right)\right]=E_{y}\left[E\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(nt)\right)|\mathcal{G}^{r}\left((n-1)t\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=E_{y}\left[E_{\hat{Y}^{r}((n-1)t)}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(t)\right)\right]\right]\leq e^{-\tilde{\delta}t}E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}((n-1)t)\right)\right]+B_{1}.

Now a standard recursive argument shows that

\displaystyle E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(T)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\tilde{\delta}nt}E_{y}\left[V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(\hat{Y}^{r}(0)\right)\right]+B_{1}\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}e^{-\tilde{\delta}lt}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\tilde{\delta}T}V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(y\right)+B_{1}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}e^{-\tilde{\delta}l\frac{1}{2}}\leq e^{-\tilde{\delta}T}V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}\left(y\right)+\frac{B_{1}}{1-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}}}.

This completes the proof on taking
B=B_{1}/(1-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}})
.
∎

8.3
Proof of Proposition
7.3

Let
\tilde{\upsilon}>0
be fixed and let
y=(\hat{q},\hat{\Upsilon},\tilde{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathcal{Y}^{r}
and
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
be arbitrary. For
T\geq 1
define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{r,T}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq r^{2}T}|A_{j}^{r}(t)-t\alpha^{r}_{j}|\leq\frac{|\theta_{i}|}{4J}rT\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq r^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}T}|S_{j}^{r}(t)-t\beta^{r}_{j}|\leq\frac{|\theta_{i}|}{4J}rT\right\}

and

\mathcal{A}^{r,T}=\cap_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{A}^{r,T}_{j}
.
From Proposition
6.2
(equations (
14
) and (
15
) with
c_{1}=1
,
c_{2}=0
) we know there exist constants
B_{1},B_{2},R\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
r\geq R
and
T\geq 1
we have

P\left(\mathcal{A}^{r,T}\right)\geq 1-B_{1}e^{-B_{2}T},\;\;r^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\beta^{r}_{j}}\leq\tilde{\upsilon},\;\;\mbox{ and, }r\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\rho^{r}_{j}-C_{i}\right)\geq\frac{3\theta_{i}}{2}.

Choose
L\in[1,\infty)
such that
1-B_{1}e^{-B_{2}L}\geq\frac{1}{2}
.
If

\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\geq 2|\theta_{i}|L+2\tilde{\upsilon},\mbox{ let }\tilde{T}\doteq\frac{1}{2|\theta_{i}|}\left(\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}-2\tilde{\upsilon}\right)

and note that
\tilde{T}\geq L
.
For all
r\geq R
and
s\in[0,\tilde{T})
on the set
\mathcal{A}^{r,\tilde{T}}
we have

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{r\beta^{r}_{j}}A^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(s-\bar{\Upsilon}_{j}^{A}\right)^{+}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{r\beta^{r}_{j}}\mathcal{I}_{\{s\geq\bar{\Upsilon}_{j}^{A}>0\}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{r\beta^{r}_{j}}S^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(\bar{B}^{r}(s)-\bar{\Upsilon}_{j}^{S}\right)^{+}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{r\beta^{r}_{j}}\mathcal{I}_{\{\bar{B}^{r}(s)\geq\bar{\Upsilon}_{j}^{S}>0\}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{r\beta^{r}_{j}}A^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(s-\bar{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\right)^{+}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\frac{1}{r\beta^{r}_{j}}S^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(s)\right)-r^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{\beta^{r}_{j}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}+r\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\rho^{r}_{j}\left(s-\bar{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\right)^{+}-r\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\bar{B}^{r}_{j}(s)+\frac{\theta_{i}}{2}\tilde{T}-\tilde{\upsilon}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}+r\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\rho^{r}_{j}\left(s-\max_{j}\{\bar{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\right)^{+}-rsC_{i}+\frac{\theta_{i}}{2}\tilde{T}-\tilde{\upsilon}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}+sr\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}K_{i,j}\rho^{r}_{j}-C_{i}\right)+\frac{\theta_{i}}{2}\tilde{T}-\tilde{\upsilon}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}+\frac{3\theta_{i}}{2}s+\frac{\theta_{i}}{2}\tilde{T}-\tilde{\upsilon}>\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}-\tilde{\upsilon}+2\theta_{i}\tilde{T}.

Since

\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}-\tilde{\upsilon}+2\theta_{i}\tilde{T}=\tilde{\upsilon}

this means that for
r\geq R
and
s\in[0,\tilde{T})
on the set
\mathcal{A}^{r,\tilde{T}}
we have
\hat{W}^{r}_{i}(s)>\tilde{\upsilon}
and consequently

\{\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}\geq\tilde{T}\}\supset\mathcal{A}^{r,\tilde{T}}
.
Therefore for
r\geq R
if
\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\geq 2|\theta_{i}|L+2\tilde{\upsilon}
we have

\displaystyle V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}(y)

\displaystyle=E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}}\right]\geq P\left(\mathcal{A}^{r,\tilde{T}}\right)e^{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-B_{1}e^{-B_{2}L}\right)e^{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{T}}\geq\frac{e^{-\frac{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{\upsilon}}{|\theta_{i}|}}}{2}e^{\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2|\theta_{i}|}\left(\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\right)}.

(61)

If
\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}<2|\theta_{i}|L+2\tilde{\upsilon}
by definition we have

V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}(y)=E_{y}\left[e^{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{\gamma}^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i,0}}\right]\geq 1
.
Let

\tilde{B}_{1}\doteq\min\left\{e^{-\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2|\theta_{i}|}\left(2|\theta_{i}|L+2\tilde{\upsilon}\right)},\frac{e^{-\frac{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{\upsilon}}{|\theta_{i}|}}}{2}\right\},\mbox{ and, }\tilde{B}_{2}\doteq\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2|\theta_{i}|}.

Then if
\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\leq 2|\theta_{i}|L+2\tilde{\upsilon}
we have

\tilde{B}_{1}e^{\tilde{B}_{2}\left(\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\right)^{+}}\leq 1\leq V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}(y)

and if
\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\geq 2|\theta_{i}|L+2\tilde{\upsilon}
we have from (
61
)

\tilde{B}_{1}e^{\tilde{B}_{2}\left(\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\right)^{+}}\leq\frac{e^{-\frac{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{\upsilon}}{|\theta_{i}|}}}{2}e^{\frac{\tilde{\delta}}{2|\theta_{i}|}\left(\hat{w}_{i}-C_{i}\max_{j}\{\hat{\Upsilon}^{A}_{j}\}\right)}\leq V^{r,\tilde{\upsilon}}_{i}(y).

The result follows.
∎

9
Proofs of Some Exponential Estimates

The following lemma gives a key independence property.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then
(\hat{A}^{r,t}(\cdot),\hat{S}^{r,t}(\cdot))
is independent of
\mathcal{G}^{r}(t)
and
(\hat{A}^{r,t}(\cdot),\hat{S}^{r,t}(\cdot))
has the same distribution as
(\hat{A}^{r}(\cdot),\hat{S}^{r}(\cdot))
.

Proof.

For
a,b\in\mathbb{N}^{J}
define

\tilde{u}^{r}_{a}(b)=\left(u^{r}_{1}(a_{1}+1)...,u^{r}_{1}(a_{1}+b_{1}),u^{r}_{2}(a_{2}+1)...,u^{r}_{2}(a_{2}+b_{2})...,u^{r}_{J}(a_{J}+1)...,u^{r}_{J}(a_{J}+b_{J})\right)

and

\tilde{v}^{r}_{a}(b)=\left(v^{r}_{1}(a_{1}+1)...,v^{r}_{1}(a_{1}+b_{1}),v^{r}_{2}(a_{2}+1)...,v^{r}_{2}(a_{2}+b_{2}),...,v^{r}_{J}(a_{J}+1)...,v^{r}_{J}(a_{J}+b_{J})\right).

It suffices to show that for all
(\tilde{n},\tilde{m})\geq(0,0)

(\tilde{u}^{r}_{\tau^{r,A}(t)}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{\tau^{r,S}(t)}(\tilde{m}))
is independent of
\mathcal{G}^{r}(t)=\mathcal{F}^{r}(\tau^{r}(t))
and
(\tilde{u}^{r}_{\tau^{r,A}(t)}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{\tau^{r,S}(t)}(\tilde{m}))
has the same distribution as
(\tilde{u}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{m}))
. Let
(n,m)\geq(0,0)
,
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\tilde{n}|_{1}+|\tilde{m}|_{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, and
G\in\mathcal{F}^{r}(\tau^{r}(t))
be arbitrary. Then

\displaystyle E\left[\mathcal{I}_{G}f\left(\tilde{u}^{r}_{\tau^{r,A}(t)}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{\tau^{r,S}(t)}(\tilde{m})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(n,m)\geq(0,0)}E\left[\mathcal{I}_{\{\tau^{r}(t)=(n,m)\}}\mathcal{I}_{G}f\left(\tilde{u}^{r}_{\tau^{r,A}(t)}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{\tau^{r,S}(t)}(\tilde{m})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(n,m)\geq(0,0)}E\left[\mathcal{I}_{G\cap\{\tau^{r}(t)=(n,m)\}}f\left(\tilde{u}^{r}_{n}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{m}(\tilde{m})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(n,m)\geq(0,0)}E\left[\mathcal{I}_{G\cap\{\tau^{r}(t)=(n,m)\}}\right]E\left[f\left(\tilde{u}^{r}_{n}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{m}(\tilde{m})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[f\left(\tilde{u}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{m})\right)\right]\sum_{(n,m)\geq(0,0)}E\left[\mathcal{I}_{G\cap\{\tau^{r}(t)=(n,m)\}}\right]=P\left(G\right)\left[f\left(\tilde{u}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{m})\right)\right]

where the third equality is from the fact that
G\cap\{\tau^{r}(t)=(n,m)\}
is
\mathcal{F}^{r}(n,m)
-measurable and
(\tilde{u}^{r}_{n}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{m}(\tilde{m}))
is independent of
\mathcal{F}^{r}(n,m)
and the fourth equality uses the fact that
(\tilde{u}^{r}_{n}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{m}(\tilde{m}))
has the same distribution as
(\tilde{u}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{n}),\tilde{v}^{r}_{0}(\tilde{m}))
.
The result follows.
∎

9.1
Proof of Proposition
7.5

Fix
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
. Define

\mathcal{F}^{r,S}_{j}(k)\doteq\sigma\left\{u^{r}_{l}(m^{u}_{l}),v^{r}_{l^{\prime}}(m^{v}_{l^{\prime}}):m^{u}_{l}\in\mathbb{N},l\in\mathbb{A}_{J},m^{v}_{l^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N},l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{A}_{J}\setminus\left\{j\right\},\text{ and }m^{v}_{j}\leq k\right\}

which is the filtration that contains the information from all inter-arrival times, all service times from queues other than the
j
-th queue, and
the first
k
service times from queue
j
. Note that

\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)
is a
\mathcal{F}^{r,S}_{j}(k)
stopping time. For all
n\geq 1
define

\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n)=\sup\left\{s\geq 0:B^{r}_{j}(s)<\sum_{l=1}^{n}v^{r}_{j}(l)\right\}

and since
B^{r}_{j}(\cdot)
is continuous we have
B^{r}_{j}\left(\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n)\right)=\sum_{l=1}^{n}v^{r}_{j}(l)
.

Define

o^{r}_{j}(n)=\inf\left\{s\geq\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n):\mathcal{E}^{r}_{j}(s)=0\right\}

and note that due to property (a) of Proposition
2.6
for all
s\in[\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n),o^{r}_{j}(n)]
we have
B^{r}_{j}\left(s\right)=\sum_{l=1}^{n}v^{r}_{j}(l)
. Define
\tilde{a}=\frac{\rho^{*}}{4}
and note that due to properties (b) and (c) of Proposition
2.6
for all
r
sufficiently large we have
\mathfrak{b}^{r}_{j}(s)\geq\tilde{a}>0
for all
s\in[o^{r}_{j}(n),\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n+1)]
.

Let
n\geq 2
be arbitrary and assume that
\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)>n-1
and
\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n
. Note that
in this case we must have
t>\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}
. Since
\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)>n-1
implies
B^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)\right)>\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}v^{r}_{j}(l)
we have

o^{r}_{j}(n-1)<r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)
because
o^{r}_{j}(n-1)\geq r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)
implies
B^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)\right)=\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}v^{r}_{j}(l)
. In addition,
\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n
implies
\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n)\geq tr^{2}
so
[r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{r^{2}\tilde{a}}\right),r^{2}t]\subset[o^{r}_{j}(n-1),\tilde{L}^{r}_{j}(n)]
and consequently
\mathfrak{b}^{r}_{j}(s)\geq\tilde{a}
for all
s\in[r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{r^{2}\tilde{a}}\right),r^{2}t]
and thus in particular
B^{r}_{j}(r^{2}t)-B^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{r^{2}\tilde{a}}\right)\right)\geq v^{r}_{j}(n)
. Therefore

\sum_{l=1}^{n}v^{r}_{j}(l)=\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}v^{r}_{j}(l)+v^{r}_{j}(n)<B^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{r^{2}\tilde{a}}\right)\right)+v^{r}_{j}(n)\leq B^{r}_{j}\left(tr^{2}\right)

which contradicts the assumption that
\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n
. Consequently

\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)>n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\right\}=\emptyset

and so

\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\right\}=\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\right\}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)>n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)>n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\right\}

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)>n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq n-1\right\}.

Note that for any
z\geq 0
,

t\geq 0
, and
n\geq 2
both
\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)>n-1\right\}
and

\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq n-1\right\}
are
\mathcal{F}^{r,S}_{j}(n-1)
-measurable and
v^{r}_{j}(n)
is independent of
\mathcal{F}^{r,S}_{j}(n-1)
.

Let
\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz)
denote the pdf of
v^{r}_{j}(1)
(recall that the
\{v^{r}_{j}(l)\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
are i.i.d.)and let
c\in(0,\delta)
be arbitrary where
\delta
is as in Condition
1
. Since
P(\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)<\infty)=1
, we have, recalling the convention
v_{j}^{r}(0)=0
,

\displaystyle E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t))}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(1)}\right]+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(n)}\mathbb{I}_{\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)=n\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(1)}\right]+\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(n)}\mathbb{I}_{\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)>n-1\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{v^{r}_{j}(n)}{r^{2}\tilde{a}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq n-1\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle=1+\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{cz}E\left[1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq n\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)>n\right\}}\right]\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz)

\displaystyle=1+\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{cz}\left(1+E\left[\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)-\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\right]\right)\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz).

(62)

Let

\hat{\tau}^{r,S,t}_{j}(z)=\min\left\{n\geq 1:\sum_{l=\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)+1}^{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)+n}v^{r}_{j}(l)\geq\frac{\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}z}{\tilde{a}}\right\}

and note that since
B^{r}_{j}(r^{2}t)-B^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq\frac{\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}z}{\tilde{a}}
and

B^{r}_{j}\left(r^{2}\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right)}v^{r}_{j}(l)
we have

\hat{\tau}^{r,S,t}_{j}(z)\geq\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t)-\tau^{r,S}_{j}\left(\left(t-\frac{z}{\tilde{a}r^{2}}\right)^{+}\right).

In addition, if we define

\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)=\min\left\{n\geq 1:\sum_{l=1}^{n}v^{r}_{j}(l)\geq\frac{\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}z}{\tilde{a}}\right\}

then we have

\hat{\tau}^{r,S,t}_{j}(z)\overset{d}{=}\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)

due to Lemma
9.1
.
Consequently, from (
62
),

E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t))}\right]\leq 1+\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{cz}\left(1+E\left[\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)\right]\right)\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz).

(63)

We will now bound
E\left[\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)\right]
from above.
For all
r
sufficiently large we have
\frac{2}{\beta_{j}}\geq\frac{1}{\beta^{r}_{j}}\geq\frac{3}{4\beta_{j}}
and
2\sigma^{v}_{j}\geq\sigma^{v,r}_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{v}_{j}
. Due to Condition
1
there exists
1\leq K<\infty
such that

\sup_{r}\left\{E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)>K\}}\right]\right\}\leq\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}
.
Therefore for all
r
sufficiently large we have

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4\beta_{j}}\leq\frac{1}{\beta^{r}_{j}}\leq E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)\leq K\}}\right]+E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)>K\}}\right]

and so

\displaystyle E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)\leq K\}}\right]\geq\frac{3}{4\beta_{j}}-E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)>K\}}\right]\geq\frac{1}{2\beta_{j}}.

In addition,

E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)\leq K\}}\right]\leq E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)<\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}\}}\right]+E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}\leq v^{r}_{j}(1)\leq K\}}\right]\\
\leq\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}P\left(v^{r}_{j}(1)<\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}\right)+KP\left(\frac{1}{4\beta_{i}}\leq v^{r}_{j}(1)\leq K\right)\leq\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}+KP\left(\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}\leq v^{r}_{j}(1)\right)

so for all
r
sufficiently large we have

P\left(\frac{1}{4\beta_{j}}\leq v^{r}_{j}(1)\right)\geq\frac{1}{K}E\left[v^{r}_{j}(1)\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(1)\leq K\}}\right]-\frac{1}{4K\beta_{j}}\geq\frac{1}{2K\beta_{j}}-\frac{1}{4K\beta_{j}}\geq\frac{1}{4K\beta_{j}}.

(64)

Define

C^{r}_{j}(n)=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}_{\{v^{r}_{j}(l)\geq\frac{1}{4K\beta_{j}}\}}

and

\zeta^{r}_{j}(z)=\min\{n\geq 0:C^{r}_{j}(n)=\lceil 4\frac{z}{\tilde{a}}K\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta_{j}\rceil\}

and note that

E\left[\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)\right]\leq E\left[\zeta^{r}_{j}(z)\right].

However, because the
\{v^{r}_{j}(l)\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
are i.i.d. it follows that
\zeta^{r}_{j}(z)
is just the sum of
\lceil 4\frac{z}{\tilde{a}}K\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta_{j}\rceil
independent geometric distributions with probability of success
p\geq\frac{1}{4K\beta_{j}}
for all
r
sufficiently large which gives

\displaystyle E\left[\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)\right]\leq E\left[\zeta^{r}_{j}(z)\right]\leq(1+4\frac{z}{\tilde{a}}K\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta_{j})4K\beta_{j}\leq 4K\beta_{j}+16\frac{z}{\tilde{a}}K^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta^{2}_{j}.

Thus, from (
63
) we have, for all
r
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle E\left[e^{cv^{r}_{j}(\tau^{r,S}_{j}(t))}\right]\leq 1+\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{cz}\left(1+E\left[\hat{\tau}^{r,S}_{j}(z)\right]\right)\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{cz}(1+4K\beta_{j}+16\frac{z}{\tilde{a}}K^{2}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\beta^{2}_{j})\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\left(1+4K\beta_{j}\right)\int_{(0,\infty)}e^{cz}\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz)+\frac{16K^{2}\beta^{2}_{j}}{\tilde{a}}\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\int_{(0,\infty)}ze^{cz}\gamma^{r}_{j}(dz).

The result follows on using Condition
1
.
∎

9.2
Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

In this section we prove the key large deviation estimates given in Proposition
6.2
which have been used on several occasions.

Fix
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
. Since the proof is identical for
A^{r}_{j}
and
S^{r}_{j}
we will only present it for
A^{r}_{j}
. Throughout in the proof we suppress the subscript
j
.
Define

C^{r}(n)\doteq\sum_{l=1}^{n}u^{r}(1)

and

\Lambda^{r}(y)=\log(E[e^{y(u^{r}(1)-\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}})}]).

Note that
y\mapsto\Lambda^{r}(y)
is infinitely differentiable for
|y|<\delta
and due to Jensen’s inequality
\Lambda^{r}(y)\geq 0
for all
|y|<\delta
. Due to Condition
1
there exists
K_{\Lambda}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{r,|y|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}}\Big|\frac{d^{3}\Lambda^{r}}{dy^{3}}(y)\Big|\leq K_{\Lambda}.

Since,
\Lambda^{r}(0)=0
,
\frac{d\Lambda^{r}}{dy}(0)=0
,
and

\frac{d^{2}\Lambda^{r}}{dy^{2}}(0)=\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}

we have for
|y|\leq\delta/2
,

\left|\Lambda^{r}(y)-\frac{y^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}\right|\leq\frac{|y|^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}.

(65)

We will first prove that, for any
c\geq 0
there exist
B_{1},B_{2},R\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
r\geq R
and
T\geq 0
we have

P\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq r^{2c}T}|A^{r}(t)-t\alpha^{r}|\geq\epsilon r^{c}T\right)=P\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-r^{2c}t\alpha^{r}|\geq\epsilon r^{c}T\right)\leq B_{1}e^{-TB_{2}}.

(66)

Due to the fact that
A^{r}
is integer-valued and the definition of
C^{r}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\left\{A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\geq r^{c}T\epsilon\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{A^{r}(r^{2c}t)\geq\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{C^{r}(\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil)\leq r^{2c}t\right\}

and

\displaystyle\left\{A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\leq-r^{c}T\epsilon\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{A^{r}(r^{2c}t)\leq\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{C^{r}(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1)>r^{2c}t\right\}.

In addition, since for all
t\in[0,T]
we have

\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil-\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}T\epsilon\right)\geq r^{2c}t

and

\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1\right)+\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}T\epsilon-1\right)\leq r^{2c}t

it follows that for all
t\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\left\{C^{r}(\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil)\leq r^{2c}t\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil-C^{r}(\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil)\geq\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}T\epsilon\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil-C^{r}(\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t+r^{c}T\epsilon\rceil)>\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}T\epsilon-1\right)\right\}

and

\displaystyle\left\{C^{r}(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1)>r^{2c}t\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1\right)-C^{r}(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1)<-\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}T\epsilon-1\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{C^{r}(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1)-\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t-r^{c}T\epsilon\rfloor+1\right)>\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}T\epsilon-1\right)\right\}.

Using these observations, we have, with

p_{c}^{r}\doteq\lceil\alpha^{r}r^{2c}T+r^{c}\epsilon T\rceil

\left\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\right)\geq r^{c}T\epsilon\right\}\subset\left\{\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)>\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\right)\right\}

(67)

and with
q_{c}^{r}=\lfloor\alpha^{r}r^{2c}T-r^{c}\epsilon T\rfloor

\left\{\inf_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\right)\leq-r^{c}T\epsilon\right\}\subset\left\{\sup_{0\leq n\leq q_{c}^{r}+1}\left(C^{r}(n)-\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n\right)>\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\right)\right\}.

(68)

For
0<y<\frac{\delta}{4}

P\Big(\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\Big)>\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}\Big(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\Big)\Big)e^{\frac{y}{\alpha^{r}}\Big(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\Big)}\leq E\Big[\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}e^{y\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\Big)}\Big].

(69)

Since
\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle E\left[\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}\left(e^{y\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}\left(e^{y\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)-n2\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}e^{n2\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{p_{c}^{r}2\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}E\left[\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}\left(e^{y\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)-n2\Lambda^{r}\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)}\right)\right]

(70)

Since
e^{\frac{y}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)-n\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}
is a nonnegative martingale and

Ee^{\frac{y}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)-n\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}<\infty

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
the Doob-Kolmogorov inequality gives

\displaystyle E\left[\sup_{0\leq n\leq p_{c}^{r}}\left(e^{y\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}n-C^{r}(n)\right)-2n\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4E\left[e^{y\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{r}}p_{c}^{r}-C^{r}(p_{c}^{r})\right)-2p_{c}^{r}\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4e^{-2p_{c}^{r}\Lambda^{r}(-\frac{y}{2})}e^{p_{c}^{r}\Lambda^{r}(-y)}.

Consequently, from (
67
), (
69
) and (
70
),

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\right)\geq r^{c}T\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-\frac{y}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\right)+p_{c}^{r}\Lambda^{r}(-y)}\leq 4e^{-\frac{y}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\right)+p_{c}^{r}\left(\frac{y^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{|y|^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-\frac{y}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\right)+\left(\alpha^{r}r^{2c}T+r^{c}\epsilon T+1\right)\left(\frac{y^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{|y|^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)},

(71)

where the second inequality is from (
65
).

Choose
R<\infty
sufficiently large that for all
r\geq R
we have
\alpha/2\leq\alpha^{r}\leq 2\alpha
,
\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}\leq 2\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}
.

Consider first the case where
c>0
.
Define

k=\epsilon/(16\left(\alpha\sigma^{u}\right)^{2})
,
and assume without loss that
R
is large enough that for all
r\geq R

r^{-c}\frac{1}{6}\alpha^{r}k^{3}K_{\Lambda}+r^{-c}\frac{1}{2}\epsilon k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+r^{-2c}\frac{1}{6}\epsilon k^{3}K_{\Lambda}\leq\frac{k\epsilon}{8\alpha},

r^{-c}\frac{k}{\alpha^{r}}+r^{-2c}\frac{1}{2}k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+r^{-3c}\frac{1}{6}k^{3}K_{\Lambda}\leq 1,\;\;\mbox{ and }kr^{-c}<\frac{\delta}{4}.

Then for all
r\geq R
, the exponent in (
71
) with
y=kr^{-c}
satisfies

\displaystyle-\frac{kr^{-c}}{\alpha^{r}}\left(r^{c}\epsilon T-1\right)+\left(\alpha^{r}r^{2c}T+r^{c}\epsilon T\right)\left(\frac{\left(kr^{-c}\right)^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{\left(kr^{-c}\right)^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\left(kr^{-c}\right)^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{\left(kr^{-c}\right)^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{k\epsilon T}{\alpha^{r}}+\frac{k}{r^{c}\alpha^{r}}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{r}Tk^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{6r^{c}}\alpha^{r}Tk^{3}K_{\Lambda}+\frac{1}{2r^{c}}\epsilon Tk^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{6r^{2c}}\epsilon Tk^{3}K_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2r^{2c}}k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{6r^{3c}}k^{3}K_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=T\left(\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{r}k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}-\frac{k\epsilon}{\alpha^{r}}\right)+T\left(r^{-c}\frac{1}{6}\alpha^{r}k^{3}K_{\Lambda}+r^{-c}\frac{1}{2}\epsilon k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+r^{-2c}\frac{1}{6}\epsilon k^{3}K_{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+r^{-c}\frac{k}{\alpha^{r}}+r^{-2c}\frac{1}{2}k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+r^{-3c}\frac{1}{6}k^{3}K_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\leq T\left(2\alpha k^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}-\frac{k\epsilon}{2\alpha}\right)+T\left(\frac{k\epsilon}{8\alpha}\right)+1=T\left(\frac{k\epsilon}{8\alpha}-\frac{k\epsilon}{2\alpha}\right)+T\left(\frac{k\epsilon}{8\alpha}\right)+1=-T\frac{k\epsilon}{4\alpha}+1.

Consequently for
r\geq R
, substituting
y=kr^{-c}
in (
71
) gives

P\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\right)\geq r^{c}T\epsilon\right)\leq 4e^{-T\frac{k\epsilon}{4\alpha}+1}\leq 4ee^{-T\frac{k\epsilon}{4\alpha}}.

Similarly, using the inequality (
68
),
an almost identical argument (which we omit for the sake of brevity) provides a similar bound on
P\left(\inf_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(r^{2c}t)-\alpha^{r}r^{2c}t\right)\leq-r^{c}T\epsilon\right)
and combining the two bounds we have the estimate in (
66
) for
c>0
.

Now consider the case
c=0
and define

q=\min\left\{1,\frac{\delta}{4},\frac{\epsilon}{8\alpha\left(\alpha\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{6}K_{\Lambda}+\epsilon\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)}\right\}.

Then for all
r\geq R
we have, for the exponent in (
71
) with
y=q
,

\displaystyle-\frac{q}{\alpha^{r}}\left(\epsilon T-1\right)+\left(\alpha^{r}T+\epsilon T\right)\left(\frac{q^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)+\left(\frac{q^{2}}{2}\left(\sigma^{u,r}\right)^{2}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\epsilon Tq}{2\alpha}+\frac{2q}{\alpha}+\alpha^{r}Tq^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\alpha^{r}T\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}+\epsilon Tq^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\epsilon T\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}+q^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\epsilon Tq}{2\alpha}+2q^{2}T\left(\alpha\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{6}K_{\Lambda}+\epsilon\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{6}K_{\Lambda}\right)+q^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{2q}{\alpha}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\epsilon Tq}{2\alpha}+\frac{\epsilon Tq}{4\alpha}+q^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{2q}{\alpha}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}=-T\frac{\epsilon q}{4\alpha}+q^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{2q}{\alpha}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}.

Consequently for
r\geq R
, substituting
y=q
in (
71
) with
c=0
, we have

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(t)-\alpha^{r}t\right)\geq T\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(4e^{q^{2}\left(\sigma^{u}\right)^{2}+\frac{2q}{\alpha}+\frac{q^{3}}{6}K_{\Lambda}}\right)e^{-T\frac{\epsilon q}{4\alpha}}.

Finally, using the inclusion in (
68
) with
c=0
we have,
by an almost identical argument (which is omitted), a similar upper bound on
P\left(\inf_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(A^{r}(t)-\alpha^{r}t\right)\leq-T\epsilon\right)
.
Combining the two bounds we now have the estimate in (
66
) for
c=0
.
We have thus proved (
14
) with
c_{2}=0
.
Now (
14
) for general
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
(and
T
replaced by
S\geq 0
) follows on taking
c=c_{1}
and
T=Sr^{c_{2}}
in (
66
). Finally, the inequality in (
16
) follows on taking
c_{1}=c_{2}=\frac{\kappa}{2}
in (
14
). ∎

9.3
Proof of Proposition
8.1
.

The proof for (
57
) is the same as that for (
56
) and so
we will only show the latter. Let
c>0
,
\epsilon>0
, and
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
be arbitrary.
Once again we suppress
j
from the notation.
Due to Condition
1
we have

\sup_{r>0}\{Ee^{\frac{3\delta}{4}v^{r}(1)}\}=\tilde{K}<\infty

where
\delta
is as in Condition
1
. Choose
R<\infty
such that for all
r\geq R
we have

\left(r^{2}+1\right)\tilde{K}e^{-r\frac{\delta}{4}c}\leq\frac{\epsilon\delta}{4}.

Consequently

\displaystyle E\left[e^{\frac{\delta}{2}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}(1)\geq rc\}}v^{r}(1)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+E\left[\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}(1)\geq rc\}}e^{\frac{\delta}{2}v^{r}(1)}\right]\leq 1+E\left[\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}(1)\geq rc\}}e^{-r\frac{\delta}{4}c}e^{\frac{3\delta}{4}v^{r}(1)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1+e^{-r\frac{\delta}{4}c}E\left[e^{\frac{3\delta}{4}v^{r}(1)}\right]\leq 1+\tilde{K}e^{-r\frac{\delta}{4}c}\leq e^{\tilde{K}e^{-r\frac{\delta}{4}c}}.

So for all
T\geq 1
and
r\geq R
we have

E\left[e^{\frac{\delta}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil r^{2}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}(l)\geq rc\}}v^{r}_{l}}\right]\leq e^{T\left(r^{2}+1\right)\tilde{K}e^{-r\frac{\delta}{4}c}}\leq e^{T\frac{\epsilon\delta}{4}}

which implies

P\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil r^{2}T\rceil}\mathcal{I}_{\{v^{r}(l)\geq rc\}}v^{r}(l)\geq\epsilon T\right)\leq e^{-\frac{\epsilon\delta}{2}T}e^{T\frac{\epsilon\delta}{4}}\leq e^{-\frac{\epsilon\delta}{4}T}.

∎

10
General Network and Cost Properties

10.1
Proof of Proposition
2.1
.

From the discussion above Proposition
2.1
, the result is clearly true when

\mathcal{C}_{K}^{h}=\text{ker}(K)
. Consider now the complementary case, namely,

\text{dim}(\mathcal{C}_{K}^{h})=\text{dim}(\text{ker}(K))-1=J-I-1
.
Let
q\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{J}
be arbitrary. For
\tilde{q}\in\Lambda\left(KMq\right)
define
\tilde{v}=(\tilde{q}-q)/\beta
.
Note that

0\leq\tilde{q}_{j}=q_{j}+(\tilde{q}_{j}-q_{j})=q_{j}+\tilde{v}_{j}\beta_{j}

for all
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
and

K\tilde{v}=KM(\tilde{q}-q)=KM\tilde{q}-KMq=0

and so
\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)
. In addition, for any
\hat{v}\in\Xi(q)
define
\hat{q}=q+\beta\hat{v}
and note that by the definition of
\Xi(q)
we have
\hat{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
and
KM\hat{q}=KM(q+\beta\hat{v})=KMq+K\hat{v}=KMq
so
\hat{q}\in\Lambda\left(KMq\right)
. Consequently
\inf_{\tilde{q}\in\Lambda(KMq)}(h\cdot\tilde{q})=\inf_{\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)}(h\cdot\left(q+\beta\tilde{v}\right))
. Therefore

\displaystyle h\cdot q-\hat{h}\left(KMq\right)=h\cdot q-\inf_{\tilde{q}\in\Lambda(KMq)}(h\cdot\tilde{q})=h\cdot q-\inf_{\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)}(h\cdot\left(q+\beta\tilde{v}\right))

\displaystyle=\sup_{\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)}(-h\cdot\left(\beta\tilde{v}\right))=\sup_{\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)}\left(-\beta h\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{J-I}u_{j}(u_{j}\cdot\tilde{v})\right)=\sup_{\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)}\left(-\beta h\cdot u_{J-I}(u_{J-I}\cdot\tilde{v})\right)

\displaystyle=|\lambda|\sup_{\tilde{v}\in\Xi(q)}\left(u_{J-I}\cdot\tilde{v}\right)=|\lambda|\tilde{d}(q),

where the last equality on the second line uses
\beta h\cdot u_{j}=0
for
j=1,\ldots J-I-1
, and the last line follows on recalling the definition of
\lambda
and
\tilde{d}
. The result follows.
∎

10.2
Proof of Proposition
2.3

Fix
z\in\chi_{J}
.
Due to the local traffic assumption (Condition
3
) for each
l\in\mathcal{A}_{z}
we can choose
s_{l}\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
such that
K_{l,s_{l}}=1
and
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}}K_{i,s_{l}}=1
. Define
S=\{s_{l}:l\in\mathcal{A}_{z}\}
,
P=\{j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}:z_{j}=1\}\setminus S
and
N=\{j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}:z_{j}=0\}\setminus S
. For
j\in N
define
v_{j}=-J
, for
j\in P
define
v_{j}=1
, and for all
l\in\mathcal{A}_{z}
define
v_{s_{l}}=-\sum_{j\neq s_{l}}K_{l,j}v_{j}
. For any
l\in\mathcal{A}_{z}
we have

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}}K_{l,j}v_{j}=\sum_{j\neq s_{l}}K_{l,j}v_{j}+v_{s_{l}}=\sum_{j\neq s_{l}}K_{l,j}v_{j}-\sum_{j\neq s_{l}}K_{l,j}v_{j}=0.

This verifies the first statement in the proposition with the above choice of
v
.
Next consider
j\in\mathbb{N}_{j}
such that
z_{j}=1
. If
j\in P
, then by definition
v_{j}>0
.
To complete the proof consider now
l\in\mathcal{A}_{z}
with
z_{s_{l}}=1
. Due to the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{z}
we have

\sum_{j:z_{j}=0}K_{l,j}\geq 1

and we also know that
K_{l,s_{i}}=0
for all
i\in\mathcal{A}_{z}\setminus\{l\}
. This implies that there exists
j^{*}\in N
such that
K_{l,j^{*}}=1
and for all
j\in\mathbb{N_{J}}\setminus\{j^{*},s_{l}\}
such that
K_{l,j}=1
, either
v_{j}=1
or
v_{j}=-J
. Since
v_{j^{*}}=-J
we have

v_{s_{l}}=-\sum_{j\neq s_{l}}K_{l,j}v_{j}=J-\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{j^{*},s_{l}\}}K_{l,j}v_{j}\geq J-(J-2)\geq 2.

Thus we have shown
that for all
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
such that
z_{j}=1
we have
v_{j}>0
. This proves the second statement in the proposition.
∎

10.3
Proofs of Propositions
7.6

and
7.7
.

We begin with two auxiliary results.

Lemma 10.1
.

There exists a
\hat{B}_{\hat{h}}^{-}\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
w^{1},w^{2}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}_{+}
satisfying
w^{1}\geq w^{2}
, we have

\hat{h}(w^{2})\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\hat{B}_{\hat{h}}^{-}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}.

Proof.

For
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
let
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\left\{j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}:K_{i,j}=1\right\}
. For
j\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
define

\mathcal{O}^{j}_{i}=\left\{l\in\mathbb{A}_{I}:K_{l,j}=1\text{ and }l\neq i\right\}
. Thus the set
\mathcal{O}^{j}_{i}
consists of all the resources that job type
j
impacts aside from resource
i
. For each
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
let
s_{i}\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
be such that
\sum_{l=1}^{I}K_{l,s_{i}}=K_{i,s_{i}}=1
. For any
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
let
e^{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
be the unit vector with one on the
j
th coordinate so
e^{j}_{j}=1
and
e^{j}_{l}=0
for
l\neq j
. Note that for any
c>0
and
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
if we define
\tilde{w}^{1}=KMc\beta_{j}e^{j}=cK_{\cdot,j}
(here
K_{\cdot,j}
is the
j
th column vector of the matrix
K
) and
\tilde{w}^{2}=KM\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{i}}c\beta_{s_{l}}e^{s_{l}}\right)
then
\tilde{w}^{2}_{l}=\tilde{w}^{1}_{l}
for
l\neq i
but
\tilde{w}^{2}_{i}=0
and
\tilde{w}^{1}_{i}=c
. In other words, by replacing
c\beta_{j}e^{j}
with
\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{i}_{j}}c\beta_{s_{l}}e^{s_{l}}
in the queue length vector we’ve reduced the workload for server
i
by
c
and we’ve changed the cost by
c\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{i}}h_{s_{l}}\beta_{s_{l}}-h_{j}\beta_{j}\right)
. This is the key idea in the proof.

Define

\tilde{R_{i}}=\max_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{i}}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{i}}h_{s_{l}}\beta_{s_{l}}-h_{j}\beta_{j}\right)^{+}\;\;\mbox{ and }\tilde{R}=\max_{i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}}\left\{\tilde{R_{i}}\right\}.

Now let
w^{1},w^{2}\in\mathbb{N}^{I}_{+}
be such that
w^{1}\geq w^{2}
and let
\tilde{q}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
satisfy
w^{1}=KM\tilde{q}^{0}
and
\hat{h}(w^{1})=h\cdot\tilde{q}^{0}
. Since
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\frac{1}{\beta_{j}}\tilde{q}^{0}_{j}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}}K_{1,j}\frac{1}{\beta_{j}}\tilde{q}^{0}_{j}=w^{1}_{1}\geq w^{1}_{1}-w^{2}_{1}
we can choose
\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}\in[0,\tilde{q}^{0}_{j}/\beta_{j}]
for all
j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
such that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}=w^{1}_{1}-w^{2}_{1}
. Define

\tilde{q}^{1}=\tilde{q}^{0}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{1}}\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}\beta_{s_{l}}e^{s_{l}}-\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}\beta_{j}e^{j}\right)

and note that
\tilde{q}^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
,

KM\tilde{q}^{1}=\left(w^{1}_{1}-\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\tilde{c}^{1}_{j},w^{1}_{2},w^{1}_{3},...,w^{1}_{I}\right)=\left(w^{2}_{1},w^{1}_{2},w^{1}_{3},...,w^{1}_{I}\right),

and

\displaystyle h\cdot\tilde{q}^{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\cdot\tilde{q}^{0}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{1}}\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}h_{s_{l}}\beta_{s_{l}}-\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}h_{j}\beta_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{h}(w^{1})+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\tilde{c}^{1}_{j}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{1}}h_{s_{l}}\beta_{s_{l}}-h_{j}\beta_{j}\right)^{+}\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}|w^{1}_{1}-w^{2}_{1}|.

Now assume that for
k\in\{1,...,I-1\}
there exists
\tilde{q}^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
such that

KM\tilde{q}^{k}=(w^{2}_{1},w^{2}_{2},...,w^{2}_{k},w^{1}_{k+1},w^{1}_{k+2},...,w^{1}_{I}),\;\;\mbox{ and }h\cdot\tilde{q}^{k}\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}\sum_{i=1}^{k}|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|.

(72)

Since
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}}\tilde{q}^{k}_{j}/\beta_{j}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}}K_{k+1,j}\tilde{q}^{k}_{j}/\beta_{j}=w^{1}_{k+1}\geq w^{2}_{k+1}
we can choose
\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}\in[0,\tilde{q}^{k}_{j}/\beta_{j}]
for all
j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}
such that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}}\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}=w^{1}_{k+1}-w^{2}_{k+1}
. Define

\tilde{q}^{k+1}=\tilde{q}^{k}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{k+1}}\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}\beta_{s_{l}}e^{s_{l}}-\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}\beta_{j}e^{j}\right).

Then, as before
\tilde{q}^{k+1}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{J}
, and from (
72
),

\displaystyle KM\tilde{q}^{k+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(w^{2}_{1},w^{2}_{2},...,w^{2}_{k},w^{1}_{k+1}-\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}}\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j},w^{1}_{k+2},...,w^{1}_{I}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(w^{2}_{1},w^{2}_{2},...,w^{2}_{k},w^{2}_{k+1},w^{1}_{k+2},...,w^{1}_{I}\right)

and

\displaystyle h\cdot\tilde{q}^{k+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\cdot\tilde{q}^{k}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{k+1}}\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}h_{s_{l}}\beta_{s_{l}}-\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}h_{j}\beta_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}\sum_{i=1}^{k}|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{k+1}}\tilde{c}^{k+1}_{j}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathcal{O}^{j}_{k+1}}h_{s_{l}}\beta_{s_{l}}-h_{j}\beta_{j}\right)^{+}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}\sum_{i=1}^{k}|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|+\tilde{R}|w^{1}_{k+1}-w^{2}_{k+1}|=\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|.

By induction this implies that there exists
\tilde{q}^{I}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
such that

KM\tilde{q}^{I}=w^{2},\mbox{ and, }h\cdot\tilde{q}^{I}\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}\sum_{i=1}^{I}|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|.

Since
\sum_{i=1}^{I}|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|\leq\sqrt{J}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}
and
\hat{h}(w^{2})\leq h\cdot\tilde{q}^{I}
due to the fact that
KM\tilde{q}^{I}=w^{2}
, we have

\hat{h}(w^{2})\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\tilde{R}\sqrt{J}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.2
.

There exists
\hat{B}_{\hat{h}}\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
w^{1},w^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{I}_{+}
we have

|\hat{h}(w^{1})-\hat{h}(w^{2})|\leq\hat{B}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}.

Proof.

Let
w^{1},w^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{I}_{+}
be arbitrary. Let
q^{*}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{J}
satisfy
KMq^{*}=w^{1}\wedge w^{2}
and
h\cdot q^{*}=\hat{h}\left(w^{1}\wedge w^{2}\right)
. Note that for every
i\in\mathbb{A}_{I}
we have
|w^{1}_{i}-w^{2}_{i}|=|w^{1}_{i}-w^{1}_{i}\wedge w^{2}_{i}|+|w^{2}_{i}-w^{1}_{i}\wedge w^{2}_{i}|
so

|w^{1}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}\leq|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2},\mbox{ and, }|w^{2}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}\leq|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2},

and

|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}\leq|w^{1}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}+|w^{2}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}.

Due to Lemma
10.1
we have

\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}\leq\hat{h}(w^{1})+\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}

and

\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})\leq\hat{h}(w^{2})+\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}|w^{2}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}\leq\hat{h}(w^{2})+\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}.

Let
\hat{B}_{\hat{h}}^{+}\in(0,\infty)
be such that for all
w\in\mathbb{R}^{I}_{+}
we have
\hat{h}(w)\leq\hat{B}_{\hat{h}}^{+}|w|_{2}
. Then, using the definition of
\hat{h}
,

\displaystyle\hat{h}(w^{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})+\hat{h}(w^{1}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2})\leq\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})+\hat{B}^{+}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{1}\wedge w^{2}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})+\hat{B}^{+}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}

and similarly

\hat{h}(w^{2})\leq\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})+\hat{B}^{+}_{\hat{h}}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}.

Consequently

|\hat{h}(w^{1})-\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})|\leq\max\{\hat{B}^{+}_{\hat{h}},\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}\}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}

and

|\hat{h}(w^{2})-\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})|\leq\max\{\hat{B}^{+}_{\hat{h}},\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}\}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}

and so

\displaystyle|\hat{h}(w^{1})-\hat{h}(w^{2})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\hat{h}(w^{1})-\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})|+|\hat{h}(w^{2})-\hat{h}(w^{1}\wedge w^{2})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max\{\hat{B}^{+}_{\hat{h}},\hat{B}^{-}_{\hat{h}}\}|w^{1}-w^{2}|_{2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

We can now complete the proof of Proposition
7.6
.

Proof of Proposition
7.6

For any
q^{1},q^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
, from Proposition
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle|\lambda|(\tilde{d}(q^{2})-\tilde{d}(q^{1}))=h\cdot q^{2}-\hat{h}(KMq^{2})-\left(h\cdot q^{1}-\hat{h}(KMq^{1})\right)

\displaystyle=h\cdot q^{2}-h\cdot q^{1}-\left(\hat{h}(KMq^{2})-\hat{h}(KMq^{1})\right)\leq h\cdot\left(q^{2}-q^{1}\right)+|\hat{h}(KMq^{2})-\hat{h}(KMq^{1})|.

The result now follows from Lemma
10.2
on observing that we can find
R\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{J}
and
r\geq R
,

|KMx|_{2}\leq 2|KM^{r}x|_{2}
.
∎

Finally we complete the proof of Proposition
7.7
.

Proof of Proposition
7.7

Let
q^{1},q^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{J}_{+}
be arbitrary. Due to Proposition
7.6
we have

\displaystyle|\lambda||\tilde{d}(q^{2})-\tilde{d}(q^{1})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|h\cdot\left(q^{2}-q^{1}\right)|+B_{\hat{h}}|KMq^{2}-KMq^{1}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|h|_{2}|q^{2}-q^{1}|_{2}+B_{\hat{h}}|KM||q^{2}-q^{1}|_{2}\leq(|h|_{2}+B_{\hat{h}}|KM|)|q^{2}-q^{1}|_{2}

which completes the proof.
∎

11
Proofs of Propositions
3.6
and
3.7

Results analogous to Propositions
3.6
and
3.7
for exponential primitives were studied in
[
6
]
and therefore we only give proof sketches here.

11.1
Proof of Proposition
3.6
.

The fact that
J^{r}_{E}(B^{r},y^{r})<\infty
for
r
sufficiently large follows from Proposition
3.4
and the fact that there exists a constant
B<\infty
such that
h\cdot q\leq B|w|
for all
q\in\Lambda(w)
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{I}_{+}
.
To prove tightness of
\{\nu^{r}\}
it is sufficient to show that the two marginals are tight. The tightness of
\nu^{r}_{(1)}
follows immediately from Proposition
3.4
. To show the tightness of the second marginal,
\nu^{r}_{(2)}(dx)
, since
x(0)=0

\nu_{(2)}^{r}
-a.s. it is sufficient to show that for any
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
there exists
\delta>0
and
R<\infty
such that for all
r\geq R
we have

E\left[\nu^{r}_{(2)}\left(\left\{\sup_{s,t\in[0,1],|s-t|<\delta}\|x(s)-x(t)\|>\epsilon_{1}\right\}\right)\right]<\epsilon_{2}.

(73)

Note that the left side above equals

\frac{1}{T_{r}}\int_{0}^{T_{r}}P\left(\sup_{s,t\in[u,u+1],|s-t|<\delta}\|\hat{X}^{r}(s)-\hat{X}^{r}(t)\|>\epsilon_{1}\right)du

and for any
t,u\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\hat{X}^{r}(u+t)=

\displaystyle\hat{X}^{r}(u)+KM^{r}\hat{A}^{r,u}\left(\left(t-\bar{\Upsilon}^{A,r}(u)\right)^{+}\right)+\frac{1}{r}KM^{r}\mathcal{I}_{\{t\geq\bar{\Upsilon}^{A,r}(u)>0\}}

\displaystyle-KM^{r}\hat{S}^{r,u}\left(\left(\bar{B}^{r}(t+u)-\bar{B}^{r}(u)-\bar{\Upsilon}^{S,r}(u)\right)^{+}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r}KM^{r}\mathcal{I}_{\{\bar{B}^{r}(t+u)-\bar{B}^{r}(u)\geq\bar{\Upsilon}^{S,r}(u)>0\}}+rtK(\rho^{r}-\rho)

\displaystyle-rK\rho^{r}\left(t\wedge\bar{\Upsilon}^{A,r}(u)\right)+rK\left((\bar{B}^{r}(t+u)-\bar{B}^{r}(u))\wedge\bar{\Upsilon}^{S,r}(u)\right).

(74)

From Proposition
6.2
and Lemma
9.1
it follows that for any
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
there exists
\delta>0
and
R<\infty
such that for all
r\geq R
and
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
we have

\frac{1}{T_{r}}\int_{0}^{T_{r}}P\left(\sup_{s,t\in[u,u+1],|s-t|<\delta}\|\hat{A}_{j}^{r,u}(s)-\hat{A}_{j}^{r,u}(t)\|>\epsilon_{1}\right)du<\epsilon_{2}

and

\frac{1}{T_{r}}\int_{0}^{T_{r}}P\left(\sup_{s,t\in[u,u+\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}],|s-t|<\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}\delta}\|\hat{S}_{j}^{r,u}(s)-\hat{S}_{j}^{r,u}(t)\|>\epsilon_{1}\right)du<\epsilon_{2}.

In addition, due to Proposition
6.1
, Proposition
7.5
, and the assumption that
\sup_{r}r\hat{\Upsilon}^{r}<\infty
it follows that for any
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
there exists
\delta>0
and
R<\infty
such that for all
r\geq R
we have

\frac{1}{T_{r}}\int_{0}^{T_{r}}P\left(\|rK\rho^{r}\bar{\Upsilon}^{A,r}(u)\|>\epsilon_{1}\right)du<\epsilon_{2}

and

\frac{1}{T_{r}}\int_{0}^{T_{r}}P\left(\|rK\bar{\Upsilon}^{S,r}(u)\|>\epsilon_{1}\right)du<\epsilon_{2}.

Also note that
rK(\rho^{r}-\rho)\rightarrow\theta
(due to Condition
2
and the paragraph that follows) and for all
s,t\geq 0
and
j\in\mathbb{A}_{J}
we have
|\bar{B}_{j}^{r}(s)-\bar{B}_{j}^{r}(t)|\leq\max_{i}\{C_{i}\}(t-s)
.
These observations, together with the form of
\|\hat{X}^{r}(s)-\hat{X}^{r}(t)\|
for
s,t\geq u
given by (
74
), give (
73
) and complete the proof.
∎

11.2
Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

Let
\nu^{*}
and
(w,x)
be as in the statement of the proposition. In what follows we let
P_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}
and
E_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}
denote probability and expectation under
\nu^{*}(\omega)
. The proof that for a.e.
\omega
,
P_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}(x\in\mathcal{C}([0,1]:\mathbb{R}^{I})=1
is the same as the proof of
[
6
, Theorem 15 part 1]
. To complete the proof of part (a) it suffices to show that for any
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{I})
(which is the space of continuous, real-valued functions on
\mathbb{R}^{I}
with compact support and continuous first and second derivatives)
f(x(t))-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(x(s))ds
is a
\sigma(w,x(s):s\leq t)
-martingale under
\nu^{*}(\omega)
for a.e
\omega
where

\mathcal{L}f(y)=\sum_{i=1}^{I}\theta_{i}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y_{i}}(y)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{I}\sum_{j=1}^{I}\Sigma_{i,j}\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}(y),\;y\in\mathbb{R}^{I}.

Let
0\leq s<t\leq 1
be fixed and let
g_{s}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{I}\times\mathcal{D}([0,s]))
and
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{I})
be arbitrary (here
\mathcal{C}_{b}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{I}\times\mathcal{D}([0,s])
denotes the space of continuous, bounded real-valued functions on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{I}\times\mathcal{D}([0,s])
).
Denoting, for
s\in[0,1]
, by
x_{s}
the restriction of
x
on
[0,s]
, we see that

g_{s}(w,x_{s})\left(f(x(t))-f(x(s))-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(x(z))dz\right)

is a bounded, continuous function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{I}\times\mathcal{D}([0,1]:\mathbb{R}^{I})
so

\displaystyle E\left[E_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}\left[g_{s}(w,x_{s})\left(f(x(t))-f(x(s))-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(x(u))du\right)\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\left[E_{\nu^{r_{m}}(\omega)}\left[g_{s}(w,x_{s})\left(f(x(t))-f(x(s))-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(x(u))du\right)\right]^{2}\right].

(75)

For
(w,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{I}_{+}\times\mathcal{D}^{I}
and
0\leq s\leq 1
, define

G^{u}_{s}(w,y)=g_{s}(w,[y(u+\cdot)-y(u)]_{s}),

where
[y(u+\cdot)-y(u)]_{s}
is the restriction of
y(u+\cdot)-y(u)
on
[0,s]
and for
0\leq s\leq t\leq 1
,

F^{u}_{s,t}(y)=f(y(u+t)-y(u))-f(y(u+s)-y(u))-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(y(u+z)-y(u))dz.

Then the expectation in (
75
) can be written as

\displaystyle E\left[\left(\frac{1}{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}G^{u}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{u}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})du\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=E\left[\frac{2}{T^{2}_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{u}G^{u}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{u}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})G^{v}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(v),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{v}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})dvdu\right]

It can be shown using Proposition
3.4
, Proposition
6.2
, Lemma
9.1
, Propositions
6.1
and
7.5
, and the assumptions that
\sup_{r}\hat{q}^{r}<\infty
and
\sup_{r}r\hat{\Upsilon}^{r}<\infty
, that

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{u\geq 0}P\left(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\bar{B}^{r}(t+u)-\bar{B}^{r}(u)-t\rho\|>\epsilon\right)=0.

(76)

The proof of the above assertion is very similar to that of
[
6
, Theorem 15 part 2 ]
and is therefore omitted.

For any
u\geq 0
define

\tilde{X}^{r,u}(t)=KM^{r}\hat{A}^{r,u}\left(t\right)-KM^{r}\hat{S}^{r,u}\left(\rho t\right)+rtK(\rho^{r}-\rho).

and

\breve{X}^{r_{m},u}(t)\doteq\begin{cases}\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(t)&\mbox{ if }u\geq t\\
\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u)+\tilde{X}^{r,u}(t-u)&\mbox{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Note that due to Lemma
9.1
the distribution of
\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u}
does not depend on
u
, meaning
\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u}\overset{d}{=}\tilde{X}^{r_{m},0}
for all
u\geq 0
, and from the central limit theorem for renewal processes (see e.g.
[
4
, Theorem 14.6]
)

\tilde{X}^{r,0}\rightarrow\hat{X}
in distribution on
\mathcal{D}^{I}
where
\hat{X}(\cdot)
is as introduced above (
10
). Since
\hat{X}\in\mathcal{C}^{I}
a.s., it follows from (
76
), Propositions
6.1
and
7.5
, and the assumption that
\sup_{r}\hat{\Upsilon}^{r}<\infty
that, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{u\geq 0}P\left(\sup_{z\in[0,1]}\|\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+z)-\breve{X}^{r_{m},u}(u+z)\|>\epsilon\right)=0.

Since
f
and it’s first and second derivatives are bounded and uniformly continuous it follows that

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{u\geq 0}E\left[\|F^{u}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})-F^{u}_{s,t}(\breve{X}^{r_{m},u+s})\|\right]=0.

(77)

and using the fact that the distribution of
\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u}
does not depend on
u
, that
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{I})
(in particular it has compact support), and that

E\left[f(\hat{X}(t-s)+x)-f(x)-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(\hat{X}(z-s)+x)dz\right]=0

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{I}
we have

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{I},u\geq 0}\left\{E\left[f(\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}(t-s)+x)-f(x)-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}(z-s)+x)dz\right]\right\}=0.

(78)

Recall that

\displaystyle F^{u}_{s,t}(\breve{X}^{r_{m},u+s})=

\displaystyle f(\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}(t-s)+\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+s)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u))-f(\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+s)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u))

\displaystyle-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}(z-s)+\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+s)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u))dz

and note that since
\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}
is independent of
\mathcal{G}^{r_{m}}(u+s)
and
\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+s)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u)
is
\mathcal{G}^{r_{m}}(u+s)
-measurable we have

\displaystyle\sup_{u\geq 0}E\left[F^{u}_{s,t}(\breve{X}^{r_{m},u+s})|\mathcal{G}^{r_{m}}(u)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{I},u\geq 0}E\left[f(\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}(t-s)+x)-f(x)-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(\tilde{X}^{r_{m},u+s}(z-s)+x)dz\right].

(79)

The fact that
F^{u}_{s,t}
and
G^{u}_{s}
are uniformly bounded in
u
gives

\displaystyle\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\frac{2}{T^{2}_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{u}G^{u}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{u}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})G^{v}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(v),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{v}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})dvdu

\displaystyle=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\frac{2}{T^{2}_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{u-1}G^{u}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{u}_{s,t}(\breve{X}^{r_{m},u+s})

\displaystyle\hskip 126.47249ptG^{v}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(v),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{v}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})dvdu

\displaystyle=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\frac{2}{T^{2}_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}\!\!\!\!\int_{0}^{u-1}\!\!E\left[F^{u}_{s,t}(\breve{X}^{r_{m},u+s})|\mathcal{G}^{r_{m}}(u+s)\right]\!G^{u}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})

\displaystyle\hskip 126.47249ptG^{v}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(v),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{v}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})dvdu=0

where the first equality comes from (
77
), and the second comes from the fact that for
u-1\geq v

G^{u}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})G^{v}_{s}(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(v),\hat{X}^{r_{m}})F^{v}_{s,t}(\hat{X}^{r_{m}})
and
\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+s)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u)
are
\mathcal{G}^{r_{m}}(u+s)
-measurable, and the third comes from (
79
) and (
78
).
Putting this all together gives

E\left[E_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}\left[g_{s}(w,x(\cdot))(f(x(t))-f(x(s))-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}f(x(u))du\right]^{2}\right]=0.

Proof of (a) now follows by a standard separability argument.

We will now prove part (b). Let
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{I})
(the space of continuous functions on
\mathbb{R}^{I}
with compact support)) and
s\in[0,1]
be arbitrary
From part (a),
P_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}(x\in\mathcal{C}([0,1]:\mathbb{R}^{I})=1
for a.e.
\omega
which implies

E\left|E_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}\left[f(w)-f(\Gamma(w+x(\cdot))(s))\right]\right|=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\left|E_{\nu^{r_{m}}(\omega)}\left[f(w)-f(\Gamma(w+x(\cdot))(s))\right]\right|.

The expectation on the right side is bounded above by

\displaystyle E\left[\left|\frac{1}{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}f(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u+s))-f(\Gamma(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u)+\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+\cdot)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u))(s))du\right|\right]

\displaystyle+E\left[\left|\frac{1}{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}f(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u))-f(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u+s))du\right|\right].

Due to Proposition
3.2
, Proposition
3.3
, Propositions
6.1
and
7.5
, the assumption that
\sup_{r}\hat{\Upsilon}^{r}<\infty
, and the fact that
f
is uniformly continuous and bounded we have

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\left|\frac{1}{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}f(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u+s))-f(\Gamma(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u)+\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u+\cdot)-\hat{X}^{r_{m}}(u))(s))du\right|=0.

In addition, since
f
is bounded and
T_{r_{m}}\rightarrow\infty
we have

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}E\left[\left|\frac{1}{T_{r_{m}}}\int_{0}^{T_{r_{m}}}[f(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u))-f(\hat{W}^{r_{m}}(u+s))]du\right|\right]=0.

Together these observations show
E\left[\left|E_{\nu^{*}(\omega)}\left[f(w)-f(\Gamma(w+x(\cdot))(s))\right]\right|\right]=0
.
By standard separability arguments it now follows that
w\overset{d}{=}\Gamma(w+x(\cdot))(s)
for all
s\in[0,1]
under
\nu^{*}(\omega)
for a.e.
\omega
which proves part (b).
∎
